*The subject who comes in analysis puts himself as such in the position of one who ignores*. *No entry possible into analysis* — never said, never thought — *without this reference, absolutely fundamental*. *It is exactly insofar as speech progresses* — insofar as the *upper pyramid* edifies itself, whose correspondence with the *three faces* (the *elaboration of Verdrängung, Verdichtung, Verneinung*) Lacan will perhaps show next time when sufficiently advanced — *that this being is realized*. Of course *absolutely non-realized at the beginning of analysis, as at the beginning of all dialectics*.

If this being exists implicitly, virtually — the *innocent*, never having entered any dialectic, *has literally no presence of this being*; he *believes himself simply in the real*. It is the *deepening of analysis by the intermediary of this revelation of this included speech, this speech revealed in a discourse expressly placed in doubt, in suspension, in parenthesis by the law of free association*, that is *the realization of this being in this discourse*.

It is insofar as analysis is *not only* what in the schema gives O and O' — *not only the reconstitution of the narcissistic image*, as it often is. If we were uniquely in *the testing of certain little behaviors more or less well-grasped, more or less astutely projected, thanks to the collaboration of these two egos* (as they tell us) here occupied to watch the surging of some *ineffable reality* — to which one could make the objection (as Lacan said) one can make for the rest of discourse: *why would this reality have anything privileged among others*?

Precisely insofar as this *discourse permits*, relative to point 0 (sometimes its image completed in 0'), *this point 0 goes somewhere else in the schema* — somewhere behind — and *insofar as his speech symbolizes it, realizes it in its being*.

### **Closing**

Lacan ends. He invites those whom this discourse has interested or worked on to pose short questions, for there is only one more session, around which he will try to organize the *conclusion* — if one can speak of conclusion — *which will serve as knot to take up next year a new chapter*.

He is more and more led to think next year he will *divide the seminar in two*: on one hand *President Schreber*, to show *the situation of the psychosis-problem* relative to this register, *what the symbolic world means in psychosis* (for that is the question); and *Das Ich und das Es*, to show how, within such a dialectic, *Freud's structuralism takes its true sense* — for literally the *ego, superego, and Es*, if they were nothing but *a renaming of the old psychological entities of passion, ego, and unconscious*, *one truly does not see what Freud would have brought new*. If these notions are usable as concepts, *valuable*, it is insofar as they *attach directly to the dialectical point* to which Lacan thinks he has led them this year, with this analysis bearing as much on the *Papers on Technique* as on the *Einführung des Narzißmus*. The three references (*Ich, Es, Überich*) are *something other than the usage commonly made of them*.

\[The audience disperses. Lacan distributes — as the closing gesture of the year — *little elephant figurines* (figurines d'éléphants) to those present, a humorous gift recalling the central pedagogical instance of his teaching this year: *the elephant brought into the room by the word "elephant"*, the symbolic act by which *what is not present is made more present than the real, by the power of the signifier*.\]

*Fin du séminaire 1953-54.*

---

## **Closing note**

This concludes the paragraph-by-paragraph commentary on Lacan's first year of seminar, *Les écrits techniques de Freud* (1953–1954). Across twenty-three sessions, Lacan has constructed the architecture of his subsequent teaching:

— the tripartition of *real, imaginary, symbolic*, introduced in the founding 1953 SIR conference, here fully articulated as the constitutive dimensions of analytic experience; — the *function of speech and language* in the analytic situation, distinguishing *full speech* (the realization of the subject's being in the symbolic) from *empty speech* (discourse caught in the imaginary play of resistances); — the *mirror stage* and the *optical schema* of the inverted bouquet/vase, articulating the *imaginary capture* of the ego and the *symbolic mediation* of recognition through the *Other*; — the *Idealich/Ich-Ideal* distinction, situating the analyst alternately at the imaginary pole of identification and the symbolic pole of recognition; — the *transference* as the *time of analysis*, the *concept of analysis*, the symbolic-temporal modulation by which the unconscious desire of the subject is brought to symbolic articulation; — the diagnostic critique of contemporary analytic technique (Anna Freud's *defense*\-analysis, Klein's *good and bad objects*, Strachey's *parasitic superego*, Balint's *two bodies' psychology* and *primary love*) as the symptom of a *theoretical lacuna* — the lacuna of the *symbolic* in post-Freudian analytic thought; — the *Freudian return*: a re-reading of Freud's *Papers on Technique*, the *Einführung des Narzißmus*, the *Metapsychological Supplement to the Theory of Dreams*, the *Wolf Man*, *Dora*, the *Rat Man*, the *Traumdeutung* (Part VII on *Übertragung*), *Die Verneinung*, and the early *Verdrängung* texts, all converging on the foundational thesis that *the unconscious is structured like a language*, that *the discourse of the unconscious is the discourse of the Other*, and that *the analyst's task is decipherment, not displacement of emotion*; — the philosophical foundations: Hegel's *master-slave dialectic*, the formula *desire is the desire of the Other*, the concept as the *time of the thing*; Augustine's *De Magistro* as the systematic phenomenology of the *symbolic and the question of truth*; Heidegger's *being-toward-death* and the *ἀλήθεια/λήθη* of every assumption into the symbolic; Sartre's *phenomenology of the look* and the *intersubjectivity of perversion*; Freud's *Wo Es war, soll Ich werden* read as *symbolic displacement* rather than *enlargement of consciousness*.

The seminar closes, fittingly, with the distribution of elephant figurines — a gesture at once warm and pedagogical, embodying in a little object made present-by-the-signifier the entire teaching of the year: that *the signifier brings into the room what the body cannot*, that *speech institutes a dimension of being beyond the real*, and that *analysis is the realization, in the symbolic, of the truth the subject has not yet been able to assume*.

---

**End of commentary.**
